{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /logs/agent/sessions/skills/enterprise-artifact-search\n\n# Enterprise Artifact Search Skill (Robust)\n\nThis skill delegates **multi-hop artifact retrieval + structured entity extraction** to a lightweight subagent, keeping the main agent’s context lean.\n\nIt is designed for datasets where a workspace contains many interlinked artifacts (documents, chat logs, meeting transcripts, PRs, URLs) plus reference metadata (employee/customer directories).\n\nThis version adds two critical upgrades:\n1) **Product grounding & anti-distractor filtering** (prevents mixing CoFoAIX/other products when asked about CoachForce).\n2) **Key reviewer extraction rules** (prevents “meeting participants == reviewers” mistake; prefers explicit reviewers, then evidence-based contributors).\n\n---\n\n## When to Invoke This Skill\n\nInvoke when ANY of the following is true:\n\n1. The question requires **multi-hop** evidence gathering (artifact → references → other artifacts).\n2. The answer must be **retrieved** from artifacts (IDs/names/dates/roles), not inferred.\n3. Evidence is scattered across multiple artifact types (docs + slack + meetings + PRs + URLs).\n4. You need **precise pointers** (doc_id/message_id/meeting_id/pr_id) to justify outputs.\n5. You must keep context lean and avoid loading large files into context.\n\n---\n\n## Why Use This Skill?\n\n**Without this skill:** you manually grep many files, risk missing cross-links, and often accept the first “looks right” report (common failure: wrong product).\n\n**With this skill:** a subagent:\n- locates candidate artifacts fast\n- follows references across channels/meetings/docs/PRs\n- extracts structured entities (employee IDs, doc IDs)\n- **verifies product scope** to reject distractors\n- returns a compact evidence map with artifact pointers\n\nTypical context savings: **70–95%**.\n\n---\n\n## Invocation\n\nUse this format:\n\n```python\nTask(subagent_type=\"enterprise-artifact-search\", prompt=\"\"\"\nDataset root: /root/DATA\nQuestion: <paste the question verbatim>\n\nOutput requirements:\n- Return JSON-ready extracted entities (employee IDs, doc IDs, etc.).\n- Provide evidence pointers: artifact_id(s) + short supporting snippets.\n\nConstraints:\n- Avoid oracle/label fields (ground_truth, gold answers).\n- Prefer primary artifacts (docs/chat/meetings/PRs/URLs) over metadata-only shortcuts.\n- MUST enforce product grounding: only accept artifacts proven to be about the target product.\n\"\"\")\n```\n\n---\n\n## Core Procedure (Must Follow)\n\n### Step 0 — Parse intent + target product\n- Extract:\n  - target product name (e.g., “CoachForce”)\n  - entity types needed (e.g., author employee IDs, key reviewer employee IDs)\n  - artifact types likely relevant (“Market Research Report”, docs, review threads)\n\nIf product name is missing in question, infer cautiously from nearby context ONLY if explicitly supported by artifacts; otherwise mark AMBIGUOUS.\n\n---\n\n### Step 1 — Build candidate set (wide recall, then filter)\nSearch in this order:\n1) Product artifact file(s): `/root/DATA/products/<Product>.json` if exists.\n2) Global sweep (if needed): other product files and docs that mention the product name.\n3) Within found channels/meetings: follow doc links (e.g., `/archives/docs/<doc_id>`), referenced meeting chats, PR mentions.\n\nCollect all candidates matching:\n- type/document_type/title contains “Market Research Report” (case-insensitive)\n- OR doc links/slack text contains “Market Research Report”\n- OR meeting transcripts tagged document_type “Market Research Report”\n\n---\n\n### Step 2 — HARD Product Grounding (Anti-distractor gate)\nA candidate report is **VALID** only if it passes **at least 2 independent grounding signals**:\n\n**Grounding signals (choose any 2+):**\nA) Located under the correct product artifact container (e.g., inside `products/CoachForce.json` *and* associated with that product’s planning channels/meetings).\nB) Document content/title explicitly mentions the target product name (“CoachForce”) or a canonical alias list you derive from artifacts.\nC) Shared in a channel whose name is clearly for the target product (e.g., `planning-CoachForce`, `#coachforce-*`) OR a product-specific meeting series (e.g., `CoachForce_planning_*`).\nD) The document id/link path contains a product-specific identifier consistent with the target product (not another product).\nE) A meeting transcript discussing the report includes the target product context in the meeting title/series/channel reference.\n\n**Reject rule (very important):**\n- If the report content repeatedly names a different product (e.g., “CoFoAIX”) and lacks CoachForce grounding → mark as DISTRACTOR and discard, even if it is found in the same file or near similar wording.\n\n**Why:** Benchmarks intentionally insert same doc type across products; “first hit wins” is a common failure.\n\n---\n\n### Step 3 — Select the correct report version\nIf multiple VALID reports exist, choose the “final/latest” by this precedence:\n\n1) Explicit “latest” marker (id/title/link contains `latest`, or most recent date field)\n2) Explicit “final” marker\n3) Otherwise, pick the most recent by `date` field\n4) If dates missing, choose the one most frequently referenced in follow-up discussions (slack replies/meeting chats)\n\nKeep the selected report’s doc_id and link as the anchor.\n\n---\n\n### Step 4 — Extract author(s)\nExtract authors in this priority order:\n1) Document fields: `author`, `authors`, `created_by`, `owner`\n2) PR fields if the report is introduced via PR: `author`, `created_by`\n3) Slack: the user who posted “Here is the report…” message (only if it clearly links to the report doc_id and is product-grounded)\n\nNormalize into **employee IDs**:\n- If already an `eid_*`, keep it.\n- If only a name appears, resolve via employee directory metadata (name → employee_id) but only after you have product-grounded evidence.\n\n---\n\n### Step 5 — Extract key reviewers (DO NOT equate “participants” with reviewers)\nKey reviewers must be **evidence-based contributors**, not simply attendees.\n\nUse this priority order:\n\n**Tier 1 (best): explicit reviewer fields**\n- Document fields: `reviewers`, `key_reviewers`, `approvers`, `requested_reviewers`\n- PR fields: `reviewers`, `approvers`, `requested_reviewers`\n\n**Tier 2: explicit feedback authors**\n- Document `feedback` sections that attribute feedback to specific people/IDs\n- Meeting transcripts where turns are attributable to people AND those people provide concrete suggestions/edits\n\n**Tier 3: slack thread replies to the report-share message**\n- Only include users who reply with substantive feedback/suggestions/questions tied to the report.\n- Exclude:\n  - the author (unless question explicitly wants them included as reviewer too)\n  - pure acknowledgements (“looks good”, “thanks”) unless no other reviewers exist\n\n**Critical rule:**\n- Meeting `participants` list alone is NOT sufficient.\n  - Only count someone as a key reviewer if the transcript shows they contributed feedback\n  - OR they appear in explicit reviewer fields.\n\nIf the benchmark expects “key reviewers” to be “the people who reviewed in the review meeting”, then your evidence must cite the transcript lines/turns that contain their suggestions.\n\n---\n\n### Step 6 — Validate IDs & de-duplicate\n- All outputs must be valid employee IDs (pattern `eid_...`) and exist in the employee directory if This document outlines the technical specifications necessary to achieve the product's objectives, including increasing user adoption by 20% and improving sales closure rates by 15% within the first year. The platform targets medium to large enterprises across various sectors, including technology, finance, healthcare, and retail.\\nArchitecture Overview: The Sales Coach platform will be hosted on a scalable cloud infrastructure provided by Amazon Web Services (AWS), ensuring high availability and performance. Key AWS services such as EC2 for compute, S3 for storage, and RDS for database management will be utilized. The architecture will be modular, allowing for seamless integration with existing CRM systems such as Salesforce and HubSpot. The platform will utilize microservices to ensure flexibility and scalability, with each service handling specific functionalities such as NLP processing, data analytics, and user management.\\nMachine Learning and NLP: The platform will leverage advanced Natural Language Processing (NLP) and machine learning technologies to provide actionable insights during sales interactions. TensorFlow and PyTorch will be used for developing and deploying machine learning models. These models will analyze sales conversations in real-time to offer feedback and coaching tips, enhancing the sales team's effectiveness. The process for updating and retraining models will involve regular data collection and analysis, followed by model retraining every quarter to incorporate new data and improve accuracy.\\nIntegration with CRM Systems: Seamless integration with major CRM systems like Salesforce and HubSpot is a critical requirement. The platform will use APIs to connect with these systems, ensuring data synchronization and a smooth user experience. This integration will allow sales teams to access insights directly within their existing workflows, promoting user adoption.\\nSecurity and Compliance: Security is a top priority for the Sales Coach platform. Data encryption will be implemented both in transit and at rest using AES-256 encryption standards to protect sensitive information. Access controls will be enforced to ensure that only authorized users can access the platform. Additionally, the platform will comply with GDPR and other relevant data protection regulations, ensuring international compliance.\\nUser Training and Support: A comprehensive training program will be developed to support user adoption. This will include onboarding sessions, detailed user manuals, and ongoing support to address any issues or questions. The training program rollout will begin in Q1 2024, with initial onboarding sessions scheduled for January, followed by monthly webinars and support sessions. The training program aims to ensure that users can effectively utilize the platform's features to improve their sales performance.\\nPerformance Metrics and Reviews: The platform's success will be measured using key performance metrics such as user adoption rates, sales closure rates, and user satisfaction. This document outlines the technical specifications necessary to achieve the product's objectives, including increasing user adoption by 20% and improving sales closure rates by 15% within the first year. The platform targets medium to large enterprises across various sectors, including technology, finance, healthcare, and retail.\\nArchitecture Overview: The Sales Coach platform will be hosted on a scalable cloud infrastructure provided by Amazon Web Services (AWS), ensuring high availability and performance. Key AWS services such as EC2 for compute, S3 for storage, and RDS for database management will be utilized. The architecture will be modular, allowing for seamless integration with existing CRM systems such as Salesforce and HubSpot. The platform will utilize microservices to ensure flexibility and scalability, with each service handling specific functionalities such as NLP processing, data analytics, and user management.\\nMachine Learning and NLP: The platform will leverage advanced Natural Language Processing (NLP) and machine learning technologies to provide actionable insights during sales interactions. TensorFlow and PyTorch will be used for developing and deploying machine learning models. These models will analyze sales conversations in real-time to offer feedback and coaching tips, enhancing the sales team's effectiveness. Data sources for model training will include anonymized sales call transcripts, CRM data, and publicly available datasets. Data quality assurance measures will involve data cleaning, normalization, and validation processes to ensure high-quality inputs. The process for updating and retraining models will involve regular data collection and analysis, followed by model retraining every quarter. The retraining steps include data preprocessing, model evaluation, hyperparameter tuning, and deployment of updated models to production environments.\\nIntegration with CRM Systems: Seamless integration with major CRM systems like Salesforce and HubSpot is a critical requirement. The platform will use APIs to connect with these systems, ensuring data synchronization and a smooth user experience. This integration will allow sales teams to access insights directly within their existing workflows, promoting user adoption. API connection maintenance will involve regular monitoring and logging to detect and resolve issues promptly. Measures for handling potential downtime include implementing fallback mechanisms and redundant API endpoints to ensure continuous service availability.\\nSecurity and Compliance: Security is a top priority for the Sales Coach platform. Data encryption will be implemented both in transit and at rest using AES-256 encryption standards to protect sensitive information. Access controls will be enforced to ensure that only authorized users can access the platform. Additionally, the platform will comply with GDPR and other relevant data protection regulations, ensuring international compliance. An incident response plan will be established to handle potential security breaches, including steps for detection, containment, eradication, recovery, and communication with stakeholders.\\nUser Training and Support: A comprehensive training program will be developed to support user adoption. This will include onboarding sessions, detailed user manuals, and ongoing support to address any issues or questions. The training program rollout will begin in Q1 2024, with initial onboarding sessions scheduled for January, followed by monthly webinars and support sessions. The training program aims to ensure that users can effectively utilize the platform's features to improve their sales performance. Targeting medium to large enterprises across various sectors, the platform aims to increase user adoption by 20% and improve sales closure rates by 15% within the first year. This document outlines the system design, detailing the architecture, components, and integration strategies to achieve these goals.\\nSystem Architecture: The platform is hosted on AWS, leveraging services such as EC2 for compute resources, S3 for storage, and RDS for relational database management. It features a modular architecture that supports seamless integration with CRM systems. The use of microservices ensures flexibility and scalability, allowing for independent deployment and scaling of components such as NLP processing and data analytics.\\nLoad Balancing Strategy: To ensure high availability and reliability, the platform employs AWS Elastic Load Balancing (ELB) to distribute incoming application traffic across multiple EC2 instances. This strategy helps to automatically scale the platform's capacity to meet varying demand levels, ensuring optimal performance and fault tolerance. ELB also provides health checks to route traffic only to healthy instances, enhancing the overall resilience of the system.\\nCommunication Between Microservices: The communication between microservices is facilitated by a message broker, specifically using AWS SQS (Simple Queue Service). This ensures reliable message delivery and decouples the services, allowing them to operate independently and scale efficiently. The message broker handles asynchronous communication, which is crucial for maintaining the responsiveness and resilience of the platform. While AWS SQS is currently used, we are exploring Apache Kafka for future scalability and more complex message processing needs.\\nTechnology Stack: Advanced NLP and machine learning technologies are employed using TensorFlow and PyTorch to provide real-time insights during sales interactions. The platform integrates with CRM systems like Salesforce and HubSpot via RESTful APIs, ensuring data synchronization and enhancing user adoption. The choice of technologies supports the platform's goals for real-time processing and scalability. Additionally, there is a commitment to continuous technology evaluation and potential upgrades to ensure the platform remains at the forefront of technological advancements.\\nSecurity: Security is a critical component of the Sales Coach platform. It employs AES-256 encryption for data protection and is fully compliant with GDPR and CCPA regulations, addressing privacy concerns in both the EU and North America. An incident response plan is in place to address potential breaches, ensuring rapid response and mitigation. Regular security audits and updates are conducted to maintain the integrity and confidentiality of user data. Additionally, the platform incorporates comprehensive monitoring and logging strategies using AWS CloudWatch and AWS CloudTrail to detect and respond to security incidents promptly.\\nUser Training and Support: User training is a key aspect of the platform's deployment strategy. It includes comprehensive onboarding sessions, detailed manuals, and ongoing support to facilitate user adoption. Training initiatives are scheduled to commence in Q4 2023, with a focus on ensuring users are well-equipped to leverage the platform's capabilities effectively in preparation for a Q2 2024 launch. A dedicated support team will be available to assist users, alongside a self-service portal that provides access to resources and troubleshooting guides, ensuring users have multiple avenues for support.\\nTesting Strategies: The platform employs rigorous testing strategies for microservices, focusing on integration and end-to-end testing. Integration testing ensures that individual microservices work together as expected, while end-to-end testing validates the entire workflow from start to finish. Automated testing frameworks are used to streamline the testing process, ensuring that new features and updates do not disrupt existing functionality.\\nPerformance Metrics: Performance metrics are crucial for tracking the platform's success. Metrics such as user adoption rates and sales closure improvements will be reviewed quarterly. This continuous monitoring will help identify areas for improvement and ensure that the platform meets its performance targets. Additionally, qualitative measures such as user satisfaction surveys will be conducted to gather feedback on user experience and identify opportunities for enhancement.\\nInternational Expansion: The Sales Coach platform is designed with international expansion in mind, targeting regions such as North America, the EU, and Asia-Pacific. It supports multiple languages to cater to a diverse user base, ensuring accessibility and usability across different markets. Initially, the platform will support English, Spanish, French, and Mandarin, with plans to expand language support based on market demand.\\nDisaster Recovery: A robust disaster recovery strategy is in place to ensure data integrity and platform availability. This includes regular data backups and automated failover mechanisms to minimize downtime and data loss in the event of a system failure. The strategy is designed to provide peace of mind to users, ensuring continuous access to the platform's features. Specific Recovery Time Objective (RTO) and Recovery Point Objective (RPO) targets are set at 4 hours and 1 hour, respectively, to ensure rapid recovery and minimal data loss.\",\n 19372→      \"date\": \"2026-06-02T08:04:00\",\n 19373→      \"author\": \"eid_99835861\",\n 19374→      \"document_link\": \"https://sf-internal.slack.com/archives/docs/latest_cofoaix_system_design_document\",\n 19375→      \"feedback\": \"- Add detailed load balancing strategy for EC2 instances.\\n- Clarify the choice of AWS SQS for message brokering and consider exploring Kafka for future scalability.\\n- Include a section on testing strategies for microservices, focusing on integration and end-to-end testing.\\n- Adjust user training timeline to start in Q4 2023 to align with a Q2 2024 launch.\\n- Expand the security section to include monitoring and logging strategies.\\n- Specify initial languages supported for international expansion to aid localization planning.\\n- Include specific RTO and RPO targets in the disaster recovery section.\\n- Add qualitative measures, such as user satisfaction surveys, to the performance metrics section.\",\n 19376→      \"type\": \"System Design Document\",\n 19377→      \"id\": \"latest_cofoaix_system_design_document\"\n 19378→    }\n 19379→  ],\n 19380→  \"meeting_transcripts\": [\n 19381→    {\n 19382→      \"transcript\": \"Attendees\\nIan Jones, Hannah Miller, George Miller, Julia Garcia, Julia Davis, George Jones, Charlie Smith\\nTranscript\\nJulia Smith: Team, I wanted to get your feedback or suggestions on the Market Research Report for CoFoAIX. Targeting industries such as e-commerce and digital marketing, this system leverages advanced AI models to enhance customer interactions. The solution is cloud-based and scalable, ensuring seamless integration with platforms like Shopify and Salesforce. This document outlines the comprehensive system design, detailing the architecture, components, and implementation strategies to achieve its objectives.\\nSystem Architecture: The architecture of Einstein Adaptive Personalization is cloud-based, utilizing microservices to ensure modularity and flexibility. This design allows for easy scaling and seamless integration with existing platforms. The system supports real-time data processing with low latency, enabling quick and accurate recommendations. Technologies such as Apache Kafka for data streaming and Apache Flink for real-time data processing are employed to achieve these capabilities. The use of microservices facilitates independent deployment and scaling of different system components, enhancing overall system performance and reliability.\\nAI Models and Algorithms: The solution employs advanced AI models, including deep learning and natural language processing, to continuously learn and adapt from user interactions. These models are designed to improve recommendation accuracy over time by analyzing user behavior and preferences. The adaptive nature of the AI ensures that the system remains relevant and effective in delivering personalized experiences. Deep learning frameworks such as TensorFlow and PyTorch are leveraged to build and train these models, ensuring high performance and scalability.\\nIntegration and APIs: Integration with platforms such as Shopify and Salesforce is facilitated through comprehensive APIs. These APIs are designed to be robust and flexible, allowing for seamless data exchange and interaction with the system. Security measures for API data exchange include the use of OAuth 2.0 for secure authentication and TLS encryption for data in transit. Detailed guides and SDKs are provided to assist developers in implementing the solution efficiently, ensuring a smooth integration process.\\nUser Interface: The user interface of Einstein Adaptive Personalization is intuitive and accessible across various devices. It features dashboards for monitoring and analytics, providing users with insights into system performance and customer interactions. The design prioritizes user experience, ensuring that the interface is easy to navigate and understand.\\nData Privacy and Security: Data privacy and security are top priorities for Einstein Adaptive Personalization. The system adheres to GDPR and CCPA regulations, implementing measures such as encryption and regular audits to protect user data. These practices ensure that customer information is handled responsibly and securely, building trust with users. An incident response plan is in place to quickly address any data breaches or security incidents, minimizing potential impacts and ensuring compliance with legal requirements.\\nTesting and Feedback: A robust testing strategy is in place to ensure system reliability and performance. This strategy incorporates user feedback to drive continuous improvement, allowing the system to evolve and adapt to changing user needs. Testing includes unit tests to verify individual components, integration tests to ensure seamless interaction between components, and user acceptance testing to validate the system's effectiveness in real-world scenarios. Regular testing and updates ensure that the solution remains effective and efficient in delivering personalized experiences.\\nImplementation Timeline: The implementation of Einstein Adaptive Personalization follows a phased approach, including strategic partnerships and pilot programs. This timeline is designed to facilitate market penetration and ensure a smooth rollout of the solution. Each phase is carefully planned to address potential challenges and optimize system performance.\\nRisk Management: Risk management strategies are in place to address potential challenges, with a focus on integration and data privacy. These strategies involve identifying potential risks, assessing their impact, and implementing measures to mitigate them. By proactively managing risks, the solution aims to maintain its integrity and effectiveness.\\nMetrics and KPIs: To measure the success of personalization features, key performance indicators (KPIs) such as customer engagement rates, conversion rates, and customer satisfaction scores are tracked. Additionally, metrics like recommendation accuracy and response time are monitored to ensure the system's effectiveness and efficiency in delivering personalized experiences.\\nConclusion: Einstein Adaptive Personalization aims to revolutionize customer engagement with its advanced technical capabilities and strategic execution plan. By leveraging AI-driven personalization, the solution enhances customer interactions, providing businesses with a powerful tool to improve customer satisfaction and loyalty.
